<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that the minimum possible maximum modulus on the unit circle of a polynomial with <i>N</i> consecutive real coefficients in $`\{-1,1\}`$ is $`(1+o(1))\sqrt N`$, as <i>N</i> tends to infinity through all integers. This disproves the real-sign analogue of Erdős's fixed relative-gap conjecture. As a consequence, the largest binary merit factor at length <i>N</i> tends to infinity through all integer lengths, disproving Turyn's conjecture.

- **stmt-thm-main** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For Littlewood polynomials with real coefficients, $$\lim_{N\to\infty}m_N=1.$$ Equivalently, for every $\eta>0$ there is $N_0$ such that for every integer $N\ge N_0$ there are signs $\varepsilon_0,\ldots,\varepsilon_{N-1}$ with $$\max_{\|z\|=1}\left\|\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\varepsilon_kz^k\right\| \le(1+\eta)\sqrt N.$$
  - Explanation: The normalized minimum maximum modulus for real-sign polynomials tends to the Parseval scale through all integer lengths.
- **stmt-prop-almost-signs** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-almost-signs)): For every $0<\delta<1/20$, there are vectors $X_N=(X_{N,0},\ldots,X_{N,N-1})\in[-1,1]^N$, one for each integer $N\ge1$, such that $$\begin{aligned} \limsup_{N\to\infty}\frac{\left\lVert Q_{X_N}\right\rVert_\infty}{\sqrt N} &\le K_\delta, &K_\delta&=\sqrt{\frac{(1+\delta)^3}{1-\delta}}, \label{eq:almost-maximum}\\ \liminf_{N\to\infty}\frac1N\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}X_{N,k}^2 &\ge1-7\delta.\label{eq:almost-energy} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Relaxed real coefficients attain an asymptotically near-Parseval maximum while retaining nearly full mean-square mass.
- **stmt-lem-rounding** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-rounding)): There is an absolute constant $C$ such that for every $N\ge1$ and every $X\in[-1,1]^N$, some signs $\varepsilon_0,\ldots,\varepsilon_{N-1}$ satisfy $$\label{eq:defect-rounding} \max_{t\in\mathbb T}\left\|\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}(\varepsilon_k-X_k)\mathrm e(kt)\right\| \le C\left(1+\sqrt{\mu(X)\log\frac{80N}{\mu(X)}}\right).$$ The square-root term is defined to be zero when $\mu(X)=0$; in that case $X$ already has sign entries and the left side may be zero.
  - Explanation: Existential signing controls the Fourier error through the total coefficient defect, with a separate zero-defect convention.
- **stmt-lem-signed-packing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-signed-packing)): Let $m\ge1$ and $r\ge2$ be integers, and let $a_1,\ldots,a_r\in\mathbb Z^m\setminus\{0\}$ be pairwise nonparallel. If $w_1,\ldots,w_r>0$ satisfy $2\sum_iw_i<1$, there exist an integer $H\ge1$ and points $\theta_1,\ldots,\theta_H\in\mathbb T^m$ such that the $2rH$ closed intervals $$I\bigl(\sigma a_i\cdot\theta_h,w_i/H\bigr), \qquad 1\le i\le r,\quad 1\le h\le H,\quad \sigma\in\{-1,1\},$$ are pairwise disjoint.
  - Explanation: Strict total width slack and pairwise nonparallel forms permit disjoint signed closed intervals across finitely many blocks.
- **stmt-thm-packing-edge-coloring** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-packing-edge-coloring)): For each integer $r\ge2$ and each $\gamma>0$, there exists $\beta>0$ with the following property. If an $r$-uniform hypergraph satisfies, for some $t>0$, $$(1-\beta)t<d(v)<(1+\beta)t\quad\text{for every vertex }v, \qquad d(u,v)<\beta t\quad\text{for }u\ne v,$$ then its edges can be partitioned into at most $(1+\gamma)t$ matchings.
  - Explanation: Near-regular uniform hypergraphs with small codegrees admit a decomposition into matchings.
- **stmt-prop-auxiliary** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-auxiliary)): For every $0<\delta<1/20$, there exist integers $m\ge1$ and $r\ge2$, nonzero vectors $a_1,\ldots,a_r\in\mathbb Z^m$, a real-valued trigonometric polynomial $F$ on $\mathbb T^m$, and a vector $v\in\mathbb R^m$ with the following properties. No two of the vectors $a_i$ are parallel, and $$\mathcal A=\{a_1,-a_1,\ldots,a_r,-a_r\}$$ is a containing Fourier support of $F$. Moreover, $$\label{eq:auxiliary-norms} \left\lVert F\right\rVert_\infty\le1, \qquad \left\lVert F\right\rVert_2\ge1-3\delta.$$ Writing $\lambda_a=a\cdot v$ for $a\in\mathcal A$, we have $$\label{eq:auxiliary-widths} \lambda_a\ne0, \qquad \sum_{a\in\mathcal A}\|\lambda_a\|<1, \qquad \frac{\|\widehat F(a)\|}{\sqrt{\|\lambda_a\|}} \le K_\delta, \qquad K_\delta=\sqrt{\frac{(1+\delta)^3}{1-\delta}}.$$ All these objects depend on $\delta$ alone.
  - Explanation: A fixed real auxiliary polynomial has nearly full norm, a signed containing support, and admissible nonzero curvature widths.
- **stmt-lem-quadratic** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-quadratic)): Let $g\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R)$ and let $\beta\in\mathbb R\setminus\{0\}$ be fixed. As $T\to\infty$ through positive real values, $$\label{eq:uniform-quadratic} \begin{split} \int_{\mathbb R}g(x)\mathrm e(T\beta x^2/2-ux)\,dx ={}&\frac{\mathrm e(\operatorname{sgn}(\beta)/8-u^2/(2T\beta))} {\sqrt{T\|\beta\|}}\\ &\qquad\cdot\left(g\left(\frac{u}{T\beta}\right) +O_{g,\beta}(T^{-1})\right), \end{split}$$ uniformly for every real $u$.
  - Explanation: Quadratic stationary phase has an error uniform in the linear frequency for a fixed smooth compactly supported amplitude.
- **stmt-lem-sampling-poisson** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-sampling-poisson)): Let $\chi\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R)$, let $x_*\in\mathbb R$ and $\lambda\in\mathbb R\setminus\{0\}$, and let $J\subset\mathbb R$ be compact. For $b\in J$ and $\ell\in\mathbb Z$, put $$x_{b,\ell}=x_*+\frac{\ell-b}{\lambda}, \qquad \omega_{b,\ell}=(b-\ell)x_* -\frac{(b-\ell)^2}{2\lambda}.$$ As $N$ tends to infinity through positive integers, uniformly for $b\in J$, $$\begin{aligned} &\frac1{\sqrt N}\sum_{k\in\mathbb Z}\chi(k/N) \mathrm e\!\left(bk+\frac{N\lambda}{2}(k/N-x_*)^2\right) \notag\\ &\qquad= \frac1{\sqrt{\|\lambda\|}} \sum_{\ell\in\mathbb Z}\chi(x_{b,\ell}) \mathrm e\!\left(\frac{\operatorname{sgn}\lambda}{8} +N\omega_{b,\ell}\right) +O(N^{-1}).\label{eq:sampling-poisson} \end{aligned}$$ The sum on the right has only finitely many nonzero terms, lying in one fixed finite set of indices for all $b\in J$. The error constant depends only on the fixed data $\chi,x_*,\lambda,J$.
  - Explanation: Poisson summation produces a uniform finite stationary expansion for a fixed smooth localized quadratic sum.
- **stmt-lem-rounding-matrix** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-rounding-matrix)): There is an absolute constant $C_0$ such that, for integers $1\le s\le R$ and a real matrix $B\in[-1,1]^{R\times s}$, there is a vector $\xi\in\{-1,1\}^s$ satisfying $$\left\lVert B\xi\right\rVert_\infty\le C_0\sqrt{s\log(2R/s)}.$$
  - Explanation: A bounded real matrix admits a sign vector with controlled row discrepancy.
- **stmt-cor-finite-flatness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-finite-flatness)): There are Littlewood polynomials $P_N$ of every length $N$ such that, for each fixed $0<p<\infty$, $$\int_{\mathbb T}\left\|\frac{\|P_N(\mathrm e(t))\|}{\sqrt N}-1\right\|^p\,dt \longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: The same sequence of normalized polynomial moduli converges in every fixed finite exponent.
- **stmt-cor-merit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-merit)): For $N\ge2$, let $$\mathcal F_N=\max_{A\in\{-1,1\}^N}F(A).$$ Then $$\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathcal F_N=+\infty,$$ where the limit runs through all integer lengths. Equivalently, one can choose a word $A_N\in\{-1,1\}^N$ for each $N\ge2$ such that $$\sum_{u=1}^{N-1}C_u(A_N)^2=o(N^2).$$
  - Explanation: The maximum binary merit factor diverges through all integer lengths, equivalently permitting subquadratic sidelobe energy.
- **stmt-cor-morse** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-morse)): There is a binary Morse shift $(X,S)$ with a unique $S$-invariant Borel probability measure $\mu$ for which the Koopman operator $U_Sg=g\circ S$ on $L^2(X,\mu)$ has simple spectrum. For the zero-coordinate function $f(x)=x(0)$, let $\sigma_f$ be the spectral measure characterized by $$\widehat{\sigma_f}(n):=\int_{\mathbb T}\mathrm e(nt)\,d\sigma_f(t) =\int_X f(x)f(S^n x)\,d\mu(x)\qquad(n\in\mathbb Z).$$ Then $d\sigma_f=h\,dt$ for some $h\in L^2(\mathbb T)$ with $h\ge0$ almost everywhere and $\int_{\mathbb T}h\,dt=1$. In particular, the absolutely continuous spectral component is nonzero.
  - Explanation: A uniquely ergodic binary Morse shift has simple spectrum and an absolutely continuous zero-coordinate spectral measure with square-integrable density.

      "exact_statement": "For Littlewood polynomials with real coefficients, $$\\lim_{N\\to\\infty}m_N=1.$$ Equivalently, for every $\\eta>0$ there is $N_0$ such that for every integer $N\\ge N_0$ there are signs $\\varepsilon_0,\\ldots,\\varepsilon_{N-1}$ with $$\\max_{|z|=1}\\left|\\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\\varepsilon_kz^k\\right|\n \\le(1+\\eta)\\sqrt N.$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-almost-signs",
        "exact_quote": "For every $0<\\delta<1/20$, there are vectors $X_N=(X_{N,0},\\ldots,X_{N,N-1})\\in[-1,1]^N$, one for each integer $N\\ge1$, such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\limsup_{N\\to\\infty}\\frac{\\left\\lVert Q_{X_N}\\right\\rVert_\\infty}{\\sqrt N}\n &\\le K_\\delta,\n &K_\\delta&=\\sqrt{\\frac{(1+\\delta)^3}{1-\\delta}},\n \\label{eq:almost-maximum}\\\\\n \\liminf_{N\\to\\infty}\\frac1N\\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}X_{N,k}^2\n &\\ge1-7\\delta.\\label{eq:almost-energy}\n\\end{aligned}$$",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-rounding",
        "exact_quote": "There is an absolute constant $C$ such that for every $N\\ge1$ and every $X\\in[-1,1]^N$, some signs $\\varepsilon_0,\\ldots,\\varepsilon_{N-1}$ satisfy $$\\label{eq:defect-rounding}\n \\max_{t\\in\\mathbb T}\\left|\\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}(\\varepsilon_k-X_k)\\mathrm e(kt)\\right|\n \\le C\\left(1+\\sqrt{\\mu(X)\\log\\frac{80N}{\\mu(X)}}\\right).$$ The square-root term is defined to be zero when $\\mu(X)=0$; in that case $X$ already has sign entries and the left side may be zero.",
        "source_id": "PM-076.3"
      },

        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-auxiliary",
        "exact_quote": "For every $0<\\delta<1/20$, there exist integers $m\\ge1$ and $r\\ge2$, nonzero vectors $a_1,\\ldots,a_r\\in\\mathbb Z^m$, a real-valued trigonometric polynomial $F$ on $\\mathbb T^m$, and a vector $v\\in\\mathbb R^m$ with the following properties. No two of the vectors $a_i$ are parallel, and $$\\mathcal A=\\{a_1,-a_1,\\ldots,a_r,-a_r\\}$$ is a containing Fourier support of $F$. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:auxiliary-norms}\n \\left\\lVert F\\right\\rVert_\\infty\\le1,\n \\qquad \\left\\lVert F\\right\\rVert_2\\ge1-3\\delta.$$ Writing $\\lambda_a=a\\cdot v$ for $a\\in\\mathcal A$, we have $$\\label{eq:auxiliary-widths}\n \\lambda_a\\ne0,\n \\qquad \\sum_{a\\in\\mathcal A}|\\lambda_a|<1,\n \\qquad\n \\frac{|\\widehat F(a)|}{\\sqrt{|\\lambda_a|}}\n \\le K_\\delta,\n \\qquad\n K_\\delta=\\sqrt{\\frac{(1+\\delta)^3}{1-\\delta}}.$$ All these objects depend on $\\delta$ alone.",
        "source_id": "PM-076.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-auxiliary",
          "exact_quote": "For every $0<\\delta<1/20$, there exist integers $m\\ge1$ and $r\\ge2$, nonzero vectors $a_1,\\ldots,a_r\\in\\mathbb Z^m$, a real-valued trigonometric polynomial $F$ on $\\mathbb T^m$, and a vector $v\\in\\mathbb R^m$ with the following properties. No two of the vectors $a_i$ are parallel, and $$\\mathcal A=\\{a_1,-a_1,\\ldots,a_r,-a_r\\}$$ is a containing Fourier support of $F$. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:auxiliary-norms}\n \\left\\lVert F\\right\\rVert_\\infty\\le1,\n \\qquad \\left\\lVert F\\right\\rVert_2\\ge1-3\\delta.$$ Writing $\\lambda_a=a\\cdot v$ for $a\\in\\mathcal A$, we have $$\\label{eq:auxiliary-widths}\n \\lambda_a\\ne0,\n \\qquad \\sum_{a\\in\\mathcal A}|\\lambda_a|<1,\n \\qquad\n \\frac{|\\widehat F(a)|}{\\sqrt{|\\lambda_a|}}\n \\le K_\\delta,\n \\qquad\n K_\\delta=\\sqrt{\\frac{(1+\\delta)^3}{1-\\delta}}.$$ All these objects depend on $\\delta$ alone.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-quadratic",
        "exact_quote": "Let $g\\in C_c^\\infty(\\mathbb R)$ and let $\\beta\\in\\mathbb R\\setminus\\{0\\}$ be fixed.         "exact_quote": "Let $\\chi\\in C_c^\\infty(\\mathbb R)$, let $x_*\\in\\mathbb R$ and $\\lambda\\in\\mathbb R\\setminus\\{0\\}$, and let $J\\subset\\mathbb R$ be compact. For $b\\in J$ and $\\ell\\in\\mathbb Z$, put $$x_{b,\\ell}=x_*+\\frac{\\ell-b}{\\lambda},\n \\qquad\n \\omega_{b,\\ell}=(b-\\ell)x_*\n                  -\\frac{(b-\\ell)^2}{2\\lambda}.$$ As $N$ tends to infinity through positive integers, uniformly for $b\\in J$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n &\\frac1{\\sqrt N}\\sum_{k\\in\\mathbb Z}\\chi(k/N)\n   \\mathrm e\\!\\left(bk+\\frac{N\\lambda}{2}(k/N-x_*)^2\\right)\n \\notag\\\\\n &\\qquad=\n \\frac1{\\sqrt{|\\lambda|}}\n \\sum_{\\ell\\in\\mathbb Z}\\chi(x_{b,\\ell})\n \\mathrm e\\!\\left(\\frac{\\operatorname{sgn}\\lambda}{8}\n                      +N\\omega_{b,\\ell}\\right)\n +O(N^{-1}).\\label{eq:sampling-poisson}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The sum on the right has only finitely many nonzero terms, lying in one fixed finite set of indices for all $b\\in J$. The error constant depends only on the fixed data $\\chi,x_*,\\lambda,J$.",
        "source_id": "PM-076.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-sampling-poisson",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Torus and exponential notation** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Write $\mathbb T=\mathbb R/\mathbb Z$ and $\mathrm e(t)=\exp(2\pi i t)$.
- **Probability Haar measure** ([source](#sec:introduction)): All tori carry probability Haar measure.
- **Littlewood polynomial** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A Littlewood polynomial of length $N$ is a polynomial $$P(z)=\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\varepsilon_k z^k, \qquad \varepsilon_k\in\{-1,1\}.$$
- **Maximum modulus** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Its maximum modulus is $\left\lVert P\right\rVert_\infty=\max_{t\in\mathbb T}\|P(\mathrm e(t))\|$.
- **Normalized minimum maximum modulus** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Define $$m_N=\min_{\varepsilon_0,\ldots,\varepsilon_{N-1}\in\{-1,1\}} \frac{1}{\sqrt N}\left\lVert \sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\varepsilon_k z^k\right\rVert_\infty.$$
- **Aperiodic correlations and merit factor** ([source](#pm-h-4)): For a binary word $A=(a_0,\ldots,a_{N-1})\in\{-1,1\}^N$ of length $N\ge2$, define its aperiodic sidelobe autocorrelations and merit factor by $$C_u(A)=\sum_{j=0}^{N-1-u}a_ja_{j+u}\quad(1\le u<N), \qquad F(A)=\frac{N^2}{2\sum_{u=1}^{N-1}C_u(A)^2}.$$
- **Fourier polynomial and defect mass** ([source](#sec:reduction)): For a vector $X=(X_0,\ldots,X_{N-1})\in[-1,1]^N$, write $$Q_X(t)=\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}X_k\mathrm e(kt),\qquad \mu(X)=\frac12\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}(1-\|X_k\|).$$
- **Closed circle interval** ([source](#sec:packing)): For $\eta\in\mathbb T$ and $0<\ell<1$, write $$I(\eta,\ell)=\{\eta+x\pmod 1:-\ell/2\le x\le\ell/2\}$$ for the closed interval with center $\eta$ and length $\ell$.
- **Fourier coefficients** ([source](#sec:spreading)): For a trigonometric polynomial $F$ on $\mathbb T^m$, write $$\widehat F(a)=\int_{\mathbb T^m}F(x)\mathrm e(-a\cdot x)\,dx, \qquad a\in\mathbb Z^m.$$
- **Containing Fourier support** ([source](#sec:spreading)): A finite set is a *containing Fourier support* if every coefficient outside it vanishes; coefficients inside it are allowed to vanish as well.
- **Binary word product** ([source](#sec:merit-morse)): For binary words $B$ and $C$ of lengths $b$ and $c$, respectively, their product is defined by $$(B\times C)(s+bt)=B(s)C(t)\qquad(0\le s<b,\ 0\le t<c).$$
- **Simple spectrum** ([source](#sec:merit-morse)): Simple spectrum means that the Koopman operator has a cyclic vector: the linear span of its integer iterates is dense in the underlying $L^2$ space.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| delta-cap | Approximation tolerance upper endpoint | < | $1/20$ | Fixed before the length tends to infinity. |
| K-delta | Normalized asymptotic maximum bound | = | $\sqrt{\frac{(1+\delta)^3}{1-\delta}}$ | Fixed tolerance; all integer lengths. |
| energy | Normalized asymptotic mean-square lower bound | ≥ | $1-7\delta$ | Liminf through integer lengths. |
| signed-width | Total signed interval width | < | $1$ | Fixed nonparallel integer forms and positive widths. |
| sampled-energy | Sampled energy product expansion | = | $1-7\delta+15\delta^2-9\delta^3$ | Fixed tolerance in the approximation regime. |
| grid-size | Fourier evaluation grid cardinality | = | $20N$ | Every positive integer length. |
| matrix-rows | Real evaluation matrix row count | = | $40N$ | Every positive integer length. |
| grid-extension | Continuous error maximum relative to grid maximum | ≤ | $\frac{G}{1-\pi\exp(1)/20}$ | The error polynomial has degree at most the length minus one. |
| determinant-lower | Curvature-product tuning lower bound | ≥ | $(1-\delta)\frac{\tau\|c_s\|^2}{\rho_s}$ | A sufficiently small positive tuning parameter is fixed. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **packing** [claim](#stmt-lem-signed-packing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-packing-edge-coloring): Random finite-field slots, conditional moment bounds, deletion and edge coloring. -> Disjoint signed closed intervals
- **quadratic** [claim](#stmt-lem-quadratic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-quadratic): Damped Gaussian integration and Fourier inversion. -> Uniform quadratic integral estimate
- **auxiliary** [claim](#stmt-prop-auxiliary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-quadratic-2): Recursive near-sign polynomial, quadratic coefficient spreading and finite truncation. -> A fixed real polynomial with admissible curvature budgets
- **poisson** [claim](#stmt-lem-sampling-poisson) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sampling-poisson): Poisson summation with a summable nonstationary tail. -> Uniform finite stationary expansion
- **sampling** [claim](#stmt-prop-almost-signs) / [proof](#eq:sampling-energy-limit): Separated stationary contributions and diagonal mean-square limits. -> Almost-sign approximation at all integer lengths
- **matrix** [claim](#stmt-lem-rounding-matrix) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rounding-matrix): Partial coloring and a convergent geometric sum of row errors. -> A sign vector with bounded row discrepancy
- **rounding** [claim](#stmt-lem-rounding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rounding-matrix-2): Mass-preserving dyadic rounding and extension of a grid bound. -> Signs with defect-sensitive Fourier error
- **main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rounding): Fix tolerance, take the length limit, then decrease tolerance. -> The normalized minimum tends to the Parseval scale
- **finite-flatness** [claim](#stmt-cor-finite-flatness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-finite-flatness): Fourth-moment squeeze and probability-space integral inequalities. -> Convergence in every fixed finite exponent
- **merit** [claim](#stmt-cor-merit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-merit): Autocorrelation Parseval identity and a fourth-moment upper bound. -> All-length divergence of maximal merit factors
- **morse** [claim](#stmt-cor-morse) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-morse): Binary Morse construction followed by Fourier Plancherel and uniqueness. -> Simple spectrum and an absolutely continuous coordinate spectral measure

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **finite-merit-identity**: For a finite binary word, the merit factor is determined exactly by its aperiodic sidelobe energy and equivalently by its fourth moment. The endpoint autocorrelation makes the denominator positive.
- **all-length-limit**: The normalized minimum maximum modulus tends to one through all integer lengths; the existence statement supplies neither a convergence rate nor an efficient signing algorithm.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **finite-asymptotic**: Treat an exact merit-factor evaluator as an efficient generator of asymptotically minimizing sign words. -> Evaluation of a supplied finite word does not establish asymptotic existence or a convergence rate.
- **uniform-lower**: Interpret the maximum-modulus estimate as two-sided uniform ultraflatness. -> The maximum estimate gives no pointwise uniform lower bound.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. The finite binary-word autocorrelation and fourth-moment identity gives an exact merit-factor evaluator. It does not construct the asymptotically minimizing words or certify the all-length limit.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of binary words.
# PRECONDITIONS: Nonempty batch, word lengths at least two, integer sign entries.
# OUTPUTS: Exact sidelobe energy and reduced merit-factor numerator and denominator per word.
# TIME: Quadratic in each word length, summed over the batch; exact integer bit costs additional.
# SPACE: Linear in batch output size plus input size; integer bit storage additional.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact fmpz and fmpq arithmetic.
# FAILURE: Invalid input raises ValueError; no approximate answers.
import json, sys
from flint import fmpz, fmpq
words = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())
if not isinstance(words, list) or not words:
    raise ValueError("nonempty batch required")
result = []
for word in words:
    if not isinstance(word, list) or len(word) < 2 or any(type(a) is not int or a not in (-1, 1) for a in word):
        raise ValueError("binary word required")
    n = len(word)
    energy = fmpz(0)
    for u in range(1, n):
        c = sum((fmpz(word[j]) * word[j+u] for j in range(n-u)), fmpz(0))
        energy += c*c
    merit = fmpq(n*n, 2*energy)
    result.append([int(energy), int(merit.numerator), int(merit.denominator)])
print(json.dumps(result, separators=(',', ':')))
```

    "oracle_code": "import json, sys\nfrom sympy import Poly, symbols, Rational\nz = symbols('z')\ndata = json.load(open(sys.argv[1]))\nanswer = []\nfor coefficients in data:\n    degree = len(coefficients)-1\n    polynomial = Poly.from_list(list(reversed(coefficients)), z)\n    reflected = Poly.from_list(coefficients, z)\n    product = polynomial * reflected\n    fourth = sum(int(a)**2 for a in product.all_coeffs())\n    squared_length = (degree+1)**2\n    sidelobes = (fourth-squared_length)//2\n    ratio = Rational(squared_length, fourth-squared_length)\n    answer.append([sidelobes, int(ratio.p), int(ratio.q)])\ntry:\n    observed = json.load(open(sys.argv[2]))\n    asserted = json.loads(sys.argv[3])\nexcept Exception:\n    sys.exit(7)\nsys.exit(0 if observed == answer == asserted else 9)\n",
    "output_contract": "JSON list of exact sidelobe energy and reduced merit-factor numerator and denominator for each supplied binary word.",
    "reason": "The finite binary-word autocorrelation and fourth-moment identity gives an exact merit-factor evaluator. It does not construct the asymptotically minimizing words or certify the all-length limit.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON list of binary words.\n# PRECONDITIONS: Nonempty batch, word lengths at least two, integer sign entries.\n# OUTPUTS: Exact sidelobe energy and reduced merit-factor numerator and denominator per word.\n# TIME: Quadratic in each word length, summed over the batch; exact integer bit costs additional.\n# SPACE: Linear in batch output size plus input size; integer bit storage additional.\n# ARITHMETIC: Exact fmpz and fmpq arithmetic.\n# FAILURE: Invalid input raises ValueError; no approximate answers.\nimport json, sys\nfrom flint import fmpz, fmpq\nwords = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())\nif not isinstance(words, list) or not words:\n    raise ValueError(\"nonempty batch required\")\nresult = []\nfor word in words:\n    if not isinstance(word, list) or len(word) < 2 or any(type(a) is not int or a not in (-1, 1) for a in word):\n        raise ValueError(\"binary word required\")\n    n = len(word)\n    energy = fmpz(0)\n    for u in range(1, n):\n        c = sum((fmpz(word[j]) * word[j+u] for j in range(n-u)), fmpz(0))\n        energy += c*c\n    merit = fmpq(n*n, 2*energy)\n    result.append([int(energy), int(merit.numerator), int(merit.denominator)])\nprint(json.dumps(result, separators=(',', ':')))\n",

| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Binary-word evaluation software can use the explicit aperiodic autocorrelation and merit-factor definitions; the asymptotic existence result does not supply a signing implementation. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: the paper supplies no game dynamics, simulation error contract, or simulation workload. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: no engineering tool interface or deployed tool contract is supplied; evaluating binary words is a possible software adaptation. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | inspirational | inferred | The real-matrix discrepancy lemma and defect-sensitive rounding suggest signing objectives under explicit input restrictions; no efficient signing algorithm is asserted. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The proof uses finite hypergraphs and vectors as mathematical objects; it supplies no data-structure operation or storage-performance contract. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: hardware mappings, accelerator arithmetic, and device-performance guarantees are not supplied. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Binary signs and low sidelobe energy do not establish a security property; the paper supplies no adversarial model or cryptographic construction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The reported computational search is context for the real-sign problem; the proved result supplies no training objective, generalisation bound, or learning algorithm. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | moderate | inspirational | inferred | The paper determines the asymptotic optimum of a binary coefficient minimax problem and supplies existential packing and discrepancy ingredients, without a finite optimisation algorithm. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: no distributed protocol, failure model, consistency property, or communication bound is established. |
| other (Binary sequence and signal evaluation) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | Binary sequence and signal evaluation can use the stated aperiodic merit factor; the all-length result gives an asymptotic existence target for low aggregate sidelobe energy. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **upper-modulus-only** (counterexample_scope): The maximum-modulus conclusion supplies no uniform lower bound throughout the circle. Blocked use: ['Claiming two-sided uniform ultraflatness or pointwise nonvanishing.']
- **existential-signing** (nonconstructive): The proof makes existential choices and asserts neither an efficient signing algorithm nor a useful convergence rate. Blocked use: ['Deriving a runtime guarantee, an explicit threshold, or a finite-length approximation guarantee from the asymptotic theorem alone.']
- **fixed-auxiliary-data** (approximation): Auxiliary dimensions, frequencies, primes, blocks, and cutoffs are fixed before the sampling length tends to infinity. Blocked use: ['Varying the auxiliary construction with the length while retaining the stated asymptotic error estimate.']
- **real-sign-class** (hypothesis): The main minimum is taken over consecutive real-sign coefficients. Blocked use: ['Transferring the minimum or its proof unchanged to an arbitrary weighted coefficient class.']
- **packing-nonparallel** (hypothesis): The signed packing requires nonzero integer vectors that are pairwise nonparallel. Blocked use: ['Applying the lemma to parallel forms or zero frequency vectors.']
- **packing-strict-slack** (hypothesis): The signed packing requires positive widths and a strict total signed-width bound. Blocked use: ['Replacing strict slack by equality or allowing nonpositive widths.']
- **relaxed-real-input** (hypothesis): The defect-sensitive rounding lemma concerns bounded real vectors. Blocked use: ['Applying its defect expression unchanged to complex or unbounded coefficients.']
- **zero-defect-convention** (edge_case): At zero defect the square-root term is defined separately, and the input already consists of signs. Blocked use: ['Evaluating the logarithmic expression at zero without the stated convention.']
- **matrix-discrepancy-input** (hypothesis): The matrix discrepancy lemma requires bounded real entries and no more columns than rows. Blocked use: ['Applying the stated discrepancy bound outside its matrix regime.']
- **sampling-fixed-regime** (approximation): The Poisson sampling remainder depends on fixed smooth compactly supported amplitude, center, nonzero curvature, and compact frequency range. Blocked use: ['Treating the remainder as uniform over varying cutoffs, vanishing curvatures, or unbounded frequency ranges.']
- **finite-exponent-flatness** (formal_scope): Modulus convergence is asserted for each fixed finite positive exponent. Blocked use: ['Inferring uniform convergence of the normalized moduli or a statement for exponents growing with length.']
- **merit-length-domain** (edge_case): The binary merit factor is used only where its sidelobe-energy denominator is positive. Blocked use: ['Assigning the same merit-factor formula to a length with an empty sidelobe sum.']
- **merit-no-rate** (nonconstructive): The all-length divergence of the largest merit factor has no quantitative rate supplied by the paper. Blocked use: ['Promising a specified merit factor at a specified length from the asymptotic corollary alone.']
- **morse-spectral-scope** (formal_scope): Simplicity concerns the full Koopman operator; absolute continuity concerns the cyclic subspace generated by the zero-coordinate function, and the remaining spectral type is unidentified. Blocked use: ['Claiming simple Lebesgue spectrum on the entire mean-zero space for the binary Morse shift.']
- **concentration-quantifier** (counterexample_scope): A bound on the uniform concentration level does not bound concentration on a prescribed set. Blocked use: ['Exchanging an infimum over sets with the value for a prescribed neighborhood.']
- **weighted-counterexample-class** (counterexample_scope): The dominant-coefficient counterexample concerns weighted coefficients rather than signs. Blocked use: ['Treating this example as a binary-sign construction.']
- **figure-schematic** (formal_scope): The interval diagram illustrates the stationary-contribution condition rather than a packing produced by the lemma. Blocked use: ['Using the diagram as a certified finite packing witness.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 076 (`lean/docs/076.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Asymptotically minimal maxima of real Littlewood polynomials](../../preprints/Asymptotically-minimal-maxima-of-real-Littlewood-polynomials-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization gives real Littlewood polynomials of every sufficiently large length whose maximum modulus on the unit circle is at most $(1+\eta)\sqrt N$ for any fixed $\eta>0$. Thus the smallest possible maximum is asymptotically minimal through all integer lengths.
>
> A further statement chooses one family of real sign polynomials for all lengths such that, for every fixed finite $p>0$, the $L^p$ mean of $\bigl||P_N(z)|/\sqrt N-1\bigr|$ on the unit circle tends to zero. The results are existential and do not give an effective convergence rate or a signing algorithm.

Comparator source `AsymptoticallyMinimalLittlewood.lean` (625 bytes, sha256 `045e02714ed642444b9257f20f1ce65d68f00e63e914cc3aa7335f6ed5648a71`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace AsymptoticallyMinimalLittlewood

def littlewoodValue {N : ℕ} (ε : Fin N → ℝ) (z : ℂ) : ℂ :=
  ∑ k : Fin N, (ε k : ℂ) * z ^ (k : ℕ)

def IsRealSigning {N : ℕ} (ε : Fin N → ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∀ k, ε k = -1 ∨ ε k = 1

def MainStatement : Prop :=
  ∀ η : ℝ, 0 < η → ∃ N₀ : ℕ, 1 ≤ N₀ ∧ ∀ N : ℕ, N₀ ≤ N →
    ∃ ε : Fin N → ℝ, IsRealSigning ε ∧
      ∀ z : ℂ, ‖z‖ = 1 → ‖littlewoodValue ε z‖ ≤ (1 + η) * Real.sqrt N

theorem main : MainStatement := by
  sorry

end AsymptoticallyMinimalLittlewood

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that the minimum possible maximum modulus on the unit circle of a polynomial with <i>N</i> consecutive real coefficients in $`\{-1,1\}`$ is $`(1+o(1))\sqrt N`$, as <i>N</i> tends to infinity through all integers. This disproves the real-sign analogue of Erdős's fixed relative-gap conjecture. As a consequence, the largest binary merit factor at length <i>N</i> tends to infinity through all integer lengths, disproving Turyn's conjecture.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index
